Dated this the 23rd day of December, 2022
J U D G M E N T
Shoba Annamma Eapen J :
These Land Acquisition Appeals are filed under Section 54 of
the Kerala Land Acquisition Act,1961 in respect of lands acquired for the
purpose of widening of Kovoor-Vellimadukunnu Road, which is a project
under Kozhikode City Road Improvement Project as per Notification

LA.App. Nos.815/2014 and connected cases
:-2-:
dated 08.07.2010. Challenging the enhanced compensation awarded
in Land Acquisition Reference cases and dissatisfied with the
compensation awarded, the State as well the claimants are in appeal.
LAA Nos. 63/2018, 275/2015, 300/2015, 321/2015, 345/2015,
365/2015, 367/2015, 384/2015, 433/2015, 434/2015, 435/2015,
438/2015, 439/2015, 446/2015, 447/2015, 453/2015, 462/2015,
13/2017, 28/2017, 513/2015, 51/2017, 68/2017, 105/2017, 198/2017,
278/2017, 285/2017, 793/2013, 179/2014, 180/2014, 200/2014,
210/2014, 217/2014, 111/2016, 245/2014, 250/2014, 279/2014,
294/2014, 300/2014, 301/2014, 310/2014, 194/2016, 319/2014,
196/2016, 206/2016, 209/2016, 335/2014, 338/2014, 343/2014,
356/2014, 357/2014, 234/2016, 260/2016, 266/2016, 267/2016,
275/2016, 286/2016, 287/2016, 291/2016, 292/2016, 293/2016,
421/2014, 429/2014, 454/2014, 455/2014, 459/2014, 461/2014,
462/2014, 463/2014, 468/2014, 472/2014, 362/2016, 365/2016,
370/2016, 371/2016, 497/2014, 374/2016, 504/2014, 380/2016,
381/2016, 382/2016, 507/2014, 383/2016, 509/2014, 386/2016,
515/2014, 521/2014, 526/2014, 534/2014, 412/2016, 415/2016,
416/2016, 417/2016, 420/2016, 423/2016, 424/2016, 579/2014,

LA.App. Nos.815/2014 and connected cases
:-3-:
586/2014, 591/2014, 600/2014, 602/2014, 613/2014, 703/2014,
713/2014, 721/2014, 722/2014, 724/2014, 729/2014, 739/2014,
747/2014, 792/2014, 794/2014, 796/2014, 797/2014, 799/2014,
800/2014, 809/2014, 810/2014, 812/2014, 813/2014, 814/2014,
815/2014, 816/2014, 817/2014, 819/2014, 820/2014, 821/2014,
822/2014, 823/2014, 824/2014, 828/2014, 831/2014, 832/2014,
833/2014, 835/2014, 842/2014, 843/2014, 844/2014, 846/2014,
847/2014, 848/2014, 849/2014, 850/2014, 851/2014, 856/2014,
857/2014, 859/2014, 871/2014, 893/2014, 898/2014, 913/2014,
261/2016 are filed by the State challenging the enhanced
compensation, Cross Objection Nos.121/2014 in LAA No.335/2014,
148/2022 in LAA No.454/2014 and 17/2022 in LAA No.429/2014 are
filed by the claimants and LAA Nos..692/2013, 693/2013, 694/2013,
701/203, 702/2013, 344/2016, 397/2017 and 398/2017 are filed by the
claimants, dissatisfied with the compensation awarded by the
Reference Court.
2. We have heard Smt.N.Sudhadevi, learned Special
Government Pleader (Land Acquisition) appearing for the State of
Kerala, Sri.K.A.Salil Narayanan, the learned Counsel appearing for the

LA.App. Nos.815/2014 and connected cases
:-4-:
appellants in L.A.A.Nos.701/2013,702/2013,692/2013,693/2013 and
694/2013, Sri.R.Bindu (Sasthamangalam), the learned counsel
appearing for the appellants in L.A.A.No.344/2016, Sri.Vinod Singh
Cherian, the learned counsel appearing for the appellants in
L.A.A.Nos.397/2017 and 398/2017, Sri.A.V.M.Salahudin, the learned
counsel appearing for the cross objector in C.O.No.121/2014 in
L.A.A.No.335/2014 and Sri.V.N.Ramesan Nambisan, the learned
counsel appearing for the cross objectors in C.O.No.148/2022 in
L.A.A.No.454/2014 and C.O.17/2022 in L.A.A.No.429/2014.
3. LAA No.815 of 2014 is treated as the main case. These
cases have come up in batches totaling to 162 appeals including cross
objections. The properties involved in all these appeals arise out of the
same notification and are adjacent properties, acquired for the very
same purpose. Hence we have heard the appeals together and are
being disposed of by this common judgment. LAA No.815/2014 (LAR
No.82/2011) is treated as the leading case and the evidence in this
case consists of oral testimony of AW1, Exts. A1 to A3, Ext.R1 to R6
and Ext.X1 and X2.

LA.App. Nos.815/2014 and connected cases
:-5-:
4. Properties in Sy.Nos.19/9,19/11,19/13,19/15,19/17,19/19,
19/21,20/5,20/7,20/9,20/12,23/15,23/16,23/18,23/22,23/26,122/4,122/5,
213/4,213/7,214/4,214/14,159/4,194/2,226/5,230/8,230/10,230/11,232/
5,234/6,234/8,234/9,234/11, 234/12, 234/13, 234/14, 236/9,
328/11,328/13,328/17,328/21, 328/23, 328/27, 329/8, 329/14, 329/15,
329/17,329/20,329/22,329/23 and 329/25 of the Chevayur Village lying
as garden lands and wet lands in the revenue records, which is in the
heart of Kovoor Town and Vellimadukunnu Town were acquired for the
purpose of widening of Vellimadukunnu-Kovoor Road, having a length
of about 2 ½ Kms, pursuant to notifications issued under Section 4(1)
of the Land Acquisition Act on 08.07.2010. The Land Acquisition
Officer fixed the land value in respect of the acquired properties ranging
from Rs.65,523/- to Rs.4,92,952/- per Are. The Reference Court
enhanced and re-fixed the value between Rs.7,41,300/- per Are
(Rs.3,00,000/- per cent) to Rs.9,88,400/- per Are (Rs.4,00,000/- per
cent). Challenging the enhancement of the land value given by the
Reference Court, the State has come up in appeal and aggrieved by
the inadequacy of the award amount, the claimants have come up in
appeal.

LA.App. Nos.815/2014 and connected cases
:-6-:
 5. Before the Reference Court, the claimants sought land value
for the acquired lands ranging from Rs.12,00,000/- to Rs.15,00,000/-
per cent and before this Court, the appellants limited their claim for land
value ranging from Rs.3,50,000/- to Rs.8,00,000/- per cent. Exts.A1 to
A3 are the main documents placed on record by the claimants in support
of their claims for enhanced amount before the Reference Court. The
State has not produced any document to rely upon. For the purpose of
the valuation of the acquired lands, the Land Acquisition Officer has
classified the lands as real garden lands, wet lands, wet land converted
into garden land and water logged land and has fixed different value to
each properties. But the Reference Court while enhancing the award
amount has classified the entire acquired properties as garden land.
 6. The learned Special Government Pleader for the State submits
that for determination of market value, the Land Acquisition officer has
verified the sale transactions that took place in the locality and adopted
the transactions effected as per the document No.2539/2009 of the
SRO, Chevayur as the basis for valuation. It is the most comparable
sale document and property acquired are small strip of lands and
building construction cannot be done in the acquired lands. It was

LA.App. Nos.815/2014 and connected cases
:-7-:
further submitted that there was wide fluctuation of land value during
the execution of Ext.A2 and A3 and that the Reference Court went
wrong in treating all the properties as a single category and refixed the
land value ranging from Rs.3,00,000/- to Rs.4,00,000/- per cent. It was
also argued that the values shown in Ext.A2 and A3 are fancy prices
and the amount awarded by the land acquisition officer was just and
reasonable.
 7. Per contra, the learned counsel for the claimants submits
that all the properties are situated within the distance of 2 ½ Km and
are in highly developed area. The properties acquired are situated In
the heart of Kovoor Town and Vellimadukunnu Town. Many important
and commercial establishments such as banks, medical college,
industrial complexes, churches, mosques, temples etc are situated near
the acquired lands. The area is well developed and is very important for
residential, industrial and commercial purposes. It was further
submitted that considering the lie, nature, importance and market value
of the properties, the compensation awarded by the Reference Court is
very low and inadequate. The learned counsel further submits that the
importance of acquired properties is more than that of the property

LA.App. Nos.815/2014 and connected cases
:-8-:
covered by Exts.A1 to A3 documents. It was also submitted that the
value fixed by the Reference Court is less and the claimants were
entitled to get Rs.3,50,000/- to Rs.8,00,000/- per cent.
 8. The first issue to be considered is whether the classification
of entire acquired lands into garden lands alone, by the Reference
Court, was correct and reasonable. The learned Special Government
Pleader appearing for the State submitted that the court below went
wrong in treating all the properties as a single category. The learned
counsel appearing for the claimants submitted that though in the village
records, some of the acquired properties are classified as wet land, all
the properties were lying as garden land at the time of acquisition. It is
an admitted fact that the acquired lands are about a length of 2 ½Km
only and are lying as a strip on both sides of the Kovoor-Vellimadukunnu Road. The Advocate Commissioner in his report has
stated that Government Law College, NGO Quarters, Chevayur Police
Station, Fire Station, Armed Reserve Police Camp, Canara Bank
Chevayur Branch, Nirmala Hospital, JDT Islam School and Polytechnic,
Government Printing Press, Juvenile Court, Madhyamam Daily Printing
Press, Medical College Calicut, Dental College Calicut, Institute of

LA.App. Nos.815/2014 and connected cases
:-9-:
Maternal and Child Health, St.Joseph's College, St.Joseph's Church,
Leprosy Hospital, Catholic Syrian Bank, Kovoor Branch, NGO Quarters
High School, Amritanandamayi Temple, Iringadanpalli Temple,
Kanhirathingal Juma Masjid etc. are within 1.5 to 2 kms radius of the
acquired property. Further, it has come out in evidence that the
acquired properties are in the heart of Kovoor Town and
Vellimadukunnu Town. The Advocate Commissioner in Ext.X1 report
has stated that all the properties, after acquisition, are lying as garden
lands and he could notice kuzhikoors including coconut trees which are
having approximate age of 10-25 years and in almost all properties,
structures are there which appear to be old. In the report, it is further
stated that the property, which is classified as water logged by the Land
Acquisition Officer, at the time of inspection, was lying as garden land.
No objections were filed to the commission report. Hence, we are of
the opinion that merely because some of the acquired properties are
classified as wetland in the village/revenue records, the acquired
properties cannot be classified as wetland and we do not find any
reason to interfere with the finding of the court below in classifying the
acquired properties as a single category, garden land.

LA.App. Nos.815/2014 and connected cases
:-10-:
 9. The next point to be considered is whether the enhancement
awarded by the Reference Court is to be set aside or whether it is just
and reasonable or it has to be modified. The basis land which the Land
Acquisition Officer adopted is Document No.2539/2009 dated
17.08.2009 of the SRO, Chevayur. The learned Special Government
Pleader submitted that the land value fixed by the Land Acquisition
Officer was reasonable and it was the prevailing rate at the time of
acquisition. It is further submitted that Exts.A1 to A3 are post-notification documents and the claimants failed to prove the price of the
lands remained static during the period between Section 4(1)
notification and dates of execution of the documents relied upon. It was
also submitted that the Advocate Commissioner has reported that the
properties covered by Ext.A2 and A3 documents are very close to
Kozhikode Town and situates around 2 ½ kms away from acquired land
and hence the enhanced value fixed by the Reference Court is highly
excessive and liable to be set aside. The learned counsel for the
claimants, on the other hand, submitted that the acquired properties
have high potential value, which lie in a most commercially, industrially,
socially and culturally important area. The property involved in Ext.A2 is

LA.App. Nos.815/2014 and connected cases
:-11-:
valued at Rs.12,00,000/- per cent and in Ext.A3 is Rs.5,25,000/- per
cent and it is further submitted that the Advocate Commissioner has
found that the market value of the property is Rs.13,00,000/- per cent
and has stated that the land involved in basis land is not similar or
similarly situated to that of the acquired land. It was thus submitted
that the value fixed by the court below is too low and unreasonable and
the claimants are entitled for an enhanced compensation.
10. The Reference Court relying on the commission report
found that the Kovoor-Vellimadukunnu road is a public road having mini
bus route and it is a corridor road connecting Calicut-Wynad Road (NH
212) and Calicut-Mavoor-Medical College Road and the acquired
properties are very near to important colleges, institutions, hospitals,
churches, banks etc. The Reference Court, on the basis of the
evidence, also found that the basis land cannot be relied upon since,
the property mentioned in the basis land is abutting a cut road and
there are no commercial or important institutions in the property
covered as per the basis land and further found that the acquired
properties are in a better position than the basis land as far as
convenience and importance is concerned. It is clear from the evidence

LA.App. Nos.815/2014 and connected cases
:-12-:
adduced and on the basis of the Advocate Commissioner's report that
though the basis land document is dated 17.08.2009, it is not
comparable to the acquired properties as far as the importance of
locality and convenience is concerned. While considering the
compensation to be awarded, it is important that the property acquired
must be comparable to the basis land document. Merely because it is a
document executed prior to the notification, the same cannot be relied
upon if the nature, lie and importance of the property is not comparable.
Moreover, the acquisition authorities have not adduced any evidence to
establish that the basis land is similar and similarly situated to the
acquired properties. Hence,we do not find any reason to interfere with
the finding of the Reference Court in not adopting the basis land value
in arriving at the market value of the acquired property.
11. Ext.A1 relied on by the claimants is the certified copy of
document No.4523/2010 dated 28.12.2010 of SRO, Chevayur, as per
which, an extent of 23.75 cents of garden land comprised in
Sy.No.178/6 of Chevayur Village has been sold for a total sale
consideration of Rs.2,85,00,000/- and the value per cent is
Rs.12,00,000/-. The Reference Court found that it is not proper to rely

LA.App. Nos.815/2014 and connected cases
:-13-:
on Ext.A1 document to fix the land value on the basis of Ext.X1 report
of the Advocate Commissioner, which states that the property is at
Malaparamba abutting Calicut-Wynad road as well as
Calicut-Ramanattukara by-pass and is about 3 kms. away from the
acquired lands. Ext.A2 is the certified copy of document No.3801/2010
dated 01.11.2010 of SRO, Chevayur. This is also a post notification
document, as per which, an extent of 10.9 cents of land has been sold
for a total sale consideration of Rs.57,22,500/- and the centage value is
Rs.5,25,000/-. The Advocate Commissioner has already reported in
Ext.X1 report that the aforesaid property is situated approximately 2
kms to the west of Iringadanpalli junction, which is a part of
Vellimadukunnu-Kovoor road. The Commissioner has further stated
that comparing to acquired lands, Ext.A2 property is closer to Calicut
city. Ext.A3 is the certified copy of document No.4079/2011 dated
31.10.2011 of SRO, Chevayur. This is also a post notification
document. An extent of 8.40 cents of land has been sold for a total sale
consideration of Rs.33,60,000/- and the centage value was
Rs.4,00,000/-. In Ext.X1 report, the Advocate Commissioner has stated
that the property covered by Ext.A3 is also situated at approximately

LA.App. Nos.815/2014 and connected cases
:-14-:
2 kms. to the west of Iringadanpally junction, which is a part of
Vellimadukunnu-Kovoor road, and comparing to the acquired lands, this
property is also closer to Calicut city.
12. The Reference Court, on the basis of evidence, found that
Exts.A1 to A3 are post notification documents and the sale of land on or
after the issue of Section 4(1) notification is stated to be the best piece
of evidence for determining the market value of the acquired land. The
Reference Court relied on Ext.A2 document as guideline for
determining the market value of the acquired land since Ext.A2
document is four months after Section 4(1) notification and thus, taking
into consideration the facts and circumstances of the case, the
reference court fixed the land value ranging from Rs.3,00,000/- to
Rs.4,00,000/- in all reference cases.
13. In Ext.X1 report of the Advocate Commissioner, who have
visited the acquired lands as well as the basis lands (the properties
covered in Exts.A1 to A3 documents), it is stated that the acquired
lands are situated in a highly developed area and the acquired lands
abut Kovoor-Vellimadukunnu road, which is a public road having mini
bus route and a corridor road connecting Calicut-Wynad road (NH 212)

LA.App. Nos.815/2014 and connected cases
:-15-:
and Calicut-Mavoor-Medical College road. It is further stated that the
Government Law College, NGO Quarters, Chevayur Police Station, Fire
Station, Armed Reserve Police Camp, Canara Bank Chevayur Branch,
Nirmala Hospital, JDT Islam School and Polytechnic, Govt. Printing
Press, Juvenile Court, Madhyamam Daily Printing Press, Calicut
Medical College, Calicut Dental College, Institute of Maternal & Child
Health, St.Joseph's College, St.Joseph's Church, Leprosy Hospital,
Catholic Syrian Bank - Kovoor Branch, NGO Quarters High School,
Amritanandamayi Temple, Iringadanpalli Temple, Kanhirathingal Juma
Masjid etc. are within 1.5 to 2 km.
14. No objection has been filed to Ext.X1 report. Since there are
no supporting documents to show the land value prior to Section 4(1)
notification, in order to determine the market value of the acquired land,
Exts.A2 & A3 and the potentiality of the land have to be taken into
consideration. The Reference Court has stated in paragraph 58 of the
common judgment in LAR No.82/2011 and connected cases that
Ext.A2 is to be treated as a guideline for determining the market value
of the acquired lands and the centage value is shown as Rs.4,00,000/-
which is factually incorrect whereas the centage value of Ext.A2

LA.App. Nos.815/2014 and connected cases
:-16-:
document is Rs.5,25,000/-. Further, it has come out in evidence as well
as on a perusal of Ext.X2 Sketch filed along with Ext.X1 commission
report, that the property near to Vellimadukunnu Junction is near to
Kozhikode-Wynad Road which is a National Highway and is having
more potential value than the property in Kovoor Junction which is near
to Calicut-Mavoor-Medical College road. Though the acquired
properties are almost 3 kms. away from the Calicut City, it is in an
important area, where there are commercial buildings, shops, banks,
hospitals, colleges, churches etc. On a perusal of Ext.X2 Sketch, it is
evident that the acquired properties are also in an important area with
easy accessibility to Calicut-Mavoor-Medical College road and Calicut-Wynad road (NH 212). It is true that the claimants could not produce
any document before the Section 4(1) notification, but, considering the
nature and lie of the acquired property, the fact that it is in a highly
important commercial area cannot be denied. Hence, while comparing
the lie and nature of the property acquired, we are of the opinion that
the property in and near Vellimadukunnu Junction has to be given more
amount. It is submitted by the learned Special Government Pleader that
the properties in respect of all the appeals and cross objection filed by
the claimants are lying within the distance of 300 metres in and from

LA.App. Nos.815/2014 and connected cases
:-17-:
Vellimadukunnu Junction towards Kovoor Junction. Since we have already
stated that the entire property is included in a single category, we find that
the amounts awarded by the Reference Court are not adequate and sufficient
and have to be modified. Hence, we are of the view that interest of justice
would be best met if the compensation amount of the properties involved in
the above appeals and cross objections filed by the claimants are refixed
and enhanced to Rs.4,50,000/- per cent. In LAA Nos.692/13, 693/13,
694/13, 701/13 the amount of compensation claimed in the appeal is only
Rs.3,50,000/- per cent and in LAA No.702/13 the amount of compensation
claimed in the appeal is only Rs.4,00,000/- per cent. In all other appeals and
cross objections filed by the claimants, the amount of compensation claimed
is Rs.4,50,000/- per cent and more.
15. The learned Special Government Pleader has submitted
that in all these appeals, advance possession was taken and awards were
passed later and hence as per Section 23(1A) of the Land Acquisition
Act, 1894, 12% additional land value can be given from the date of
Sec.4(1) Notification till the date of taking possession of the properties.
The Reference Court has given 12% additional land value from the date
of Sec.4(1) notification till the date of award which is wrong. We find
force in the argument. On verification of the records and awards, it is

LA.App. Nos.815/2014 and connected cases
:-18-:
seen that the dates of awards are subsequent to the dates of taking
possession of the land. Hence, the claimants are entitled to get 12%
additional land value from the date of Section 4(1) notification till the
date of taking possession of the land as per Section 23(1A) of the Land
Acquisition Act, 1894.
16. In the result, the Land Acquisition appeals are disposed of
as follows:
(a) In LAA Nos.692/13, 693/13, 694/13, 701/13, 702/13,
344/16, 397/17 and 398/17 and C.O No.121/14 in
L.A.A.No.335/2014 filed by the claimants, the acquired
properties are lying within the distance of 300 metres from
Vellimadukunnu Junction towards Kovoor Junction and in
C.O.No.17/22 in L.A.A.No.429/2014 and C.O.No.148/2022
in L.A.A.No.454/2014, the acquired properties are lying in
Vellimadukunnu Junction, on either side of Vellimadukunnu-Kovoor road and the compensation amount awarded by the
Reference Court in all these aforementioned cases are
enhanced and refixed to Rs.4,50,000/- per cent with all
consequential statutory benefits as per law, including costs.

LA.App. Nos.815/2014 and connected cases
:-19-:
LAA Nos.692/13, 693/13, 694/13, 701/13, 702/13, 344/16,
397/17 and 398/17 and C.O No.121/14 in L.A.A.No.
335/2014 are allowed with all consequential statutory
benefits as per law, including costs and C.O.Nos.17/22 in
L.A.A.No.429/2014 & 148/22 in L.A.A.No.454/2014 filed by
the claimants are partly allowed with all consequential
statutory benefits as per law, including costs. All interlocutory
applications are closed.
 (b) The appeals filed by the State are partly allowed and the
judgments and decrees of the Reference Court in those
appeals, except in the appeals and the cross objections filed
by the claimants, are confirmed. It is made clear that in all
these appeals, including the appeals and the cross
objections filed by the claimants, they are entitled to get 12%
additional land value from the date of Notification under
Section 4(1) of the Land Acquisition Act till the date of taking
possession of the land.
(c) In LAA Nos.692/13, 693/13, 694/13, 701/13 and 702/13, the
appellants/cross objectors have limited the claim for land

LA.App. Nos.815/2014 and connected cases
:-20-:
value to less than Rs.4,50,000/- per cent. It is made clear
that they will be entitled to claim the enhanced amount of
Rs.4,50,000/- per cent stated in clause(a), only on payment
of deficit court fee for the differential amount, in view of the
decision of the Hon'ble Supreme Court in Chandrasekhar
and others v. Additional Special Land Acquisition
Officer ((2009) 14 SCC 441) within a period of two weeks
from the date of receipt of a certified copy of this judgment,
failing which the claimants/cross objectors herein, will be
entitled for enhancement of the compensation only to the
extent which they have claimed in the respective appeals
or cross objections. In all the other appeals and cross
objections filed by the claimants, they will be entitled for the
enhanced amount as stated above in Clause (a).
The Registry shall take note of specific orders, if any
contained in any of the appeals and cross-objections with
respect to exemption of payment of interest for the period of

LA.App. Nos.815/2014 and connected cases
:-21-:
 delay condoned, through specific orders passed in the
respective delay condonation applications, while drafting
the decree in respect of such cases.
sd/-
